# Decision Registry — Disclosure Drift

**Purpose:** a single index of every record in `Docs/Decisions/`, its current status, and any
supersession relationship between records. This registry does not itself change any frozen
definition. It reports what the existing decision records already say.

**Created:** 2026-07-27, during the M2.3 governance-repair exception (Decision 010 audit finding
C10: "No decision registry exists"; corrected-plan §7.5). Authority for creating this file is the
scoped documentation exception described in `Milestones/milestone_2_3_pilot_selection_plan.md`
§"Documentation authorization note".

**How to use this registry:** when two records appear to address the same topic, the record with
the later **Supersedes**/**Superseded by** relationship controls for the specific point it names.
A supersession is always partial unless the superseding record says otherwise — it never implies
the earlier record is wrong or withdrawn on any point it does not name.

(2) M3.2 becomes **two sequential windows** — M3.2A bootstrap, then transport disabled, objects frozen, dependent references **derived** from them, a second zero-request plan, and a **second exact owner approval**, then M3.2B — with **the 10% contingency withdrawn**; (3) **M3.3A** implements the candidate-snapshot builder and rehearses execution offline before **M3.3B** freezes anything real; (4) **M3.4 always requires a bounded contract and is never documentary** — M3.4A validates a minimal approval-recording entry point against synthetic catalogs, M3.4B invokes it once after explicit approval, and **manual SQL against the real catalog is prohibited**; (6) `A_max = 12` and `planned × 12` are **withdrawn** — maximum reachable physical attempts is **derived per route from the implemented response-policy state machine and independently tested**; (7) one receipt integrity identity only, `receipt_id = SHA256(canonical bytes with receipt_id omitted)`, with `receipt_content_sha256` **removed**; (8) every receipt field classified **required / conditionally required / prohibited by invocation mode**; (9) `rehearsal` and `dry_run` receipts report **zero actual network counts**, with simulated totals in the rehearsal evidence report; (10) a **two-layer evidence model** — the public repository tracks blank templates, planning records, the limitations register, and a new **evidence index** carrying artifact type, phase, status, SHA-256, and a non-sensitive reference identifier, while **completed operational evidence lives in an owner-controlled private evidence root outside the repository**; and (11) the claim that **any regeneration necessarily creates a new root is withdrawn as false** — unchanged governed state plus byte-identical canonical serialization produces the **same** root, an independently re-derived identical root **remains the same approved value**, and only a differing root, changed governed state, or a superseding manifest requires a new packet. **v0.2 changes no formal outcome, no acceptance, no phase in the Decision 024 §5.1 map, and no prohibition**, and creates no second numbered decision. **Next authorized action: `INDEPENDENT_M3_MASTER_PLAN_REREVIEW`** — and only after that fresh focused review passes may the owner authorize a separate session to draft the bounded M3.1 implementation contract |

Accepts the Decision 063 resolver result (`M3_2_DECISION_063_CROSS_NAMESPACE_RECEIPT_CHAIN_IMPLEMENTATION_OWNER_ACCEPTED`) and adjudicates every remaining known M3.2 recovery defect. **§2** corrects condition 8.12: the carry-in cross-check compares the checkpoint against the receipt chain's **ROOT** facts — including the root's own `request_plan_sha256` — never the head's, because a carry-in is minted at the chain's first attempt while an accepted plan transition lawfully moves a later head onto a successor plan; condition **8.10** still asks about the head. The rule is general and no plan hash is special-cased. **§3** extends condition 8.2 to establish a successful `complete` terminal head from the same ten durable conditions every other terminal head is held to, without fabricating an interruption state and without calling a completed window a failure. **§4** separates **evidence certainty** from **continuation permission**: `SAFE` never means permission to acquire again, a `complete` head is explicitly non-resumable, the continuation proposal refuses it on its own rather than through an empty remainder, and the live driver re-asserts the refusal adjacent to the transport-construction site so a resume against a complete receipt constructs no network. **§5** replaces the blanket determination prerequisite for `rebuild-projection` alone with an explicit eleven-condition action-specific eligibility gate, removing the circularity in which the projection lag blocked its own repair; the gate is **stricter**, adding the requirement that the mismatch be a deterministic reconstruction (absent, empty, byte-exact prefix, stale flags, or the durable rebuild-owed marker) rather than a **divergence** (disagreeing bytes, reordering, duplicate or unknown identity, malformed or truncated line, appended garbage, unsafe path), which is a deliberate narrowing of the action; a new bounded offline driver is **permitted in contract scope** and is **not authorized to be implemented or executed**. **§5 Ruling R14** forbids using a uniformly empty `schema_fingerprint_sha256` merely because the parse layer was never run: only a **legitimate** zero-structural-row parse result may use the accepted empty-row-set digest, a failed source is **never** converted into a fabricated empty structural set, fingerprints must be recomputable from the **actual** authorized parse result, and construction **refuses** if required structural evidence is missing at its accepted evidence floor. **§6 Ruling R15** selects **ALT-3**: Decision 016 §4 is retained **exactly** — `evidence_sha256` keeps `source_observation_id` and `parsed_record_id`, with no removal and no surrogate — because the M3.3 operation is deterministic over the frozen accepted observation rows and reacquisition is prohibited; the residual **cross-reacquisition non-invariance** is recorded as limitation **D067-L1**, grants **no** acquisition authority, and is **not repaired**. **§7 Ruling R16** fixes the previously ungoverned identities: `evidence_sha256` at accepted `hash_table` domain `pilot_candidate_evidence_row` over exactly the eight Decision-016 §4 fields, hashing `canonical_observed_value` in the canonical representation **already produced for persistence** with **no second normalization** and **no second hashing implementation**, excluding `evidence_id`, `snapshot_id`, the parent key, `recorded_at_utc`, `detail`, `census_run_id`, paths, physical SQLite bytes, and approval/publication state — **content identity, not row uniqueness**; and all **eight** candidate `*_resolution_sha256` columns as a **candidate-layer** digest that never reuses the census accession `resolution_sha256`, computed as `contributing_evidence_sha256 = hash_table("pilot_candidate_resolution_evidence", (evidence_role, precedence, evidence_sha256), <the exact contributing rows>, deterministically ordered)` then `hash_table("pilot_candidate_resolution", (classification_dimension, contributing_evidence_sha256, evidence_policy_version, resolved_value), <one canonical row>)`, failing closed where a required resolved value cannot be established and exempting none of the five analogue-less dimensions; tie-break hashes keep their accepted definitions. **§8** records for the first time four **previously frozen** owner dispositions — **OQ-3** fail closed on a same-catalog `snapshot_id` collision with no `INSERT OR REPLACE`, no `INSERT OR IGNORE`, and no silent recognize-and-return; AC power was confirmed, a per-process `caffeinate -i -m -s` assertion is required against `sleep=1`/`disksleep=10`, and persistent `pmset` settings must not be altered. **§10 (D126-R8)** the proposed run ID `m3_3_d126_complete_first_source_v1` and its command are **NOT frozen**, because D127 changes the code baseline; | **The M3.2 historical orphan-adoption procedure — its architecture, exact write contract, content rulings, preflight, terminal delta, fault semantics, synthetic-proof and evidence contracts, and execution/recovery boundary** | **Decision 057 — `ACCEPTED — OWNER APPROVED 2026-08-09`; binding, and explicitly non-self-executing.** The controlling record for **how the single historical orphan may later be adopted**, filling the gap Decision 055 §9 (Path B) deliberately left open by neither designing the adoption in executable detail nor performing it. `ObservationRecorder.record` is itself **prohibited**, because it opens its own transaction and would nest a second `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` inside `CatalogWriter.batch`, and the guards are **one-use refusals** with no update, upsert, or replay path); the **content rulings** (hardcoded verifier `detail` and `outcome='stored_new'` accepted unchanged, **zero** observation-reason, archive-member, recovery-state, receipt, checkpoint, attempt, and run-registration rows); the **thirteen-gate conjunctive fail-closed preflight, sequenced by §7.1** (A read-only gates → B acquire the writer lease → C create and verify the source-bound snapshot under it → D lease-continuity recheck → E artifact digest and identity re-verification → F transaction 1, with the lease held **unbroken** from B through F so no uncontrolled interval exists in which another writer could mutate the source), including **zero `resolution_state='blocked'` rows catalog-wide** — a deliberately stronger gate than the code's path-scoped checks, because the resolution `UPDATE` is path-scoped and would silently resolve a pre-existing incident; the **exact terminal delta** and the expectation that the receiptless determination is **`UNSAFE` solely for absence of a predecessor receipt**, never `SAFE`, never resumption; the **six-point fault classification** with **no claim of end-to-end atomicity**; **sixteen synthetic cases** — cases 1–15 preserved and unrenumbered, case 16 additive and proving gate 6's catalog-wide refusal on a `blocked` recovery event at a **different** `projection_path`, the one preflight gate with no code backstop — non-vacuous for **every behaviourally reachable** row-shape, timestamp, flag, and nested-transaction mutation — including a contrast proving the excluded `reconcile`/`quarantine` path would **move** a disposable fixture object, and a case that must fail if §5.1's **row construction** is mutated or removed, while the `cursor.rowcount == 1` guard is instead a **directly asserted and evidenced invariant** whose deletion is expressly **not** required to be caught by a mutation (a permitted plain `INSERT` yields `1`; ## Open items this registry surfaces but does not resolve

- **The index skips 102.** Decision 102 was an owner-side determination about the interrupted M3.3-E0 run — that PID `43427` is dead, that no E0 process, catalog writer, or advisory-lock holder remains, and that the persisted `held` lease is stale interruption residue no existing governed mechanism could reconcile. It was issued as an owner finding and never committed as a record under `Docs/Decisions/`, so there is no `decision_102_*.md` file. [Decision 103](decision_103_m3_3_e0_interruption_recovery.md) §1 restates those findings as accepted entry state and is the committed record that carries them. The gap is expected, not a missing file. **Accepted [Decision 106](decision_106_m3_3_recovery_implementation_acceptance_and_preflight_authorization.md) §2 disposes of it as finding F7 — an accepted documentation/numbering gap on which no runtime behaviour and no controlling authority depends.**

- **The index skips 128.** There is **no `decision_128_*.md` file**, and there never was one. `D128` names a **run identity** — the complete-first-source canary world `m3_3_d128_complete_first_source_v1` — rather than a decision record, and that run was launched under an owner instrument issued outside this repository. [Decision 129](decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §2 states this, and Decision 129 is the **first governance record of D128** — its semantic adjudication. [Decision 130](decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) is the second, recording the archival of that world to the `SSK SSD` and its verified internal deletion; **neither file is a `decision_128_*.md` and neither closes the numbering gap**. The gap is expected, not a missing file, and it is the same shape as the Decision 102 gap recorded above.

- As of 2026-07-27: Decision 001 remains "Accepted working decision; final literature refresh still
  required" and Decision 004 remains "Proposed freeze pending project-owner approval" in their own
  files. This registry reports that status verbatim; it does not grant or withhold approval on
  their behalf. Decision 002 was approved and clarified on 2026-07-27 (see Index) and is no longer
  in this pending set. Anyone relying on Decision 004 (evaluation protocol) as *approved* should
  confirm current status directly, since this registry is a point-in-time index and the decision
  files are the source of truth.
- `decision_003_temporal_split.md` (unsuffixed, live) and `decision_003_temporal_split_v0_2.md`
  (archived) held byte-identical Decision 003 v0.2 content **before** the 2026-07-27
  governance-repair pass. Only the live file was then given the Decision 010 supersession banner,
  since it is the file named without a version suffix and is therefore treated as the live record.
  The archived `_v0_2` copy was left untouched and remains the unmodified historical snapshot of
  what the live file said before that banner was added.
